### §4 Decisions and questions asked of Taylor

- Mo. Rev. Stat. § 700.600 (120 days' notice before tenants who own their homes must leave a land lease community "due to a change in use of the property"): not built into owner-occupancy-termination-mo or environmental-event-termination-mo, because an owner moving in or an environmental event ending a lease is not a change in use of the community; a landlord changing the use of a community must follow § 700.600 whatever the lease says (edu-term-change-notice-mo states it).
- Item 9 arbitration (Taylor's rule, 2026-11-07): not offered for Missouri; `edu-binding-arbitration-mo` explains why. Reasoning in batch-1 DECISIONS below. Not asked of Taylor (the prompt said not to).
- courts.mo.gov: the first read of Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 55.03 returned only the page shell (the rule-text script was refused with a 403 after a Cloudflare challenge request), and I asked Taylor in chat to clear a bot check. Taylor saw no check; a retry at 17:21 MT loaded the rule normally, so nothing was worked around. Rule 55.03(a) read and saved; `edu-entity-landlord-representation-mo` closed VERIFIED with the cautious course.
- Item 6, `keys` and `parking-vehicle-rules` fee charges: confirmed lawful as written. G106 (whole-code search, 6 hits; rerun of G104, which failed its real positive and is recorded as failed, rerun as G106) found no rule that a fee must be stated in the lease to be collectible; the only residential hit is the carpet-cleaning proviso of Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.300(4)(2). Neither clause withholds from the deposit; withholding stays limited to the three purposes of Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.300(4), which `security-deposit-use-mo` states (an unpaid decal charge is not one of them; an unreturned-key re-key cost is arguably restoring the unit, case law not read, and the clauses do not claim it). The Merchandising Practices Act reaches leases, but a fee the lease states and the landlord actually incurs is not an unfair practice on the face of 15 CSR 60-8.020; no conflict.
- Item 6, `parking-vehicle-rules` towing for expired registration: confirmed; no narrower sentence needed. Mo. Rev. Stat. § 304.157(4) lets the owner or lessee in possession tow 'abandoned property or property parked in a restricted or assigned area' without an officer only when present and with the entrance sign or after notifying police and waiting 10 or 96 hours; nothing in Mo. Rev. Stat. § 304.157 or Mo. Rev. Stat. § 304.001(1) bars a tow because registration has expired, and the clause tows only 'in accordance with applicable law'. The existing MO segment already says this. `parking-vehicle-rules-id` not needed.
- Item 7, `addendum-precedence`: confirmed. Its carve-out ('any disclosure, notice, or addendum that applicable law requires ... will control') keeps `landlord-disclosure-mo` (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.185), `meth-disclosure-mo` (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.236), `contamination-disclosure-mo` (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 442.055) and the federal lead disclosure (FEDERAL: 42 U.S.C. § 4852d) controlling.
- Item 8(b), `holdover-ca`: confirmed against Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.080 (double the yearly value only for a tenant who 'shall willfully hold over' after 'demand made and notice in writing given'): the clause claims actual damages and reasonable rental value and leaves the statutory double measures to the statute, as its MO segment says. `holdover-rate-mo`'s exclusion ('other than a termination for nonpayment of Rent') confirmed present.
- Item 8(d), CARES: confirmed that none of `edu-nonpayment-demand-mo`, `edu-cure-grounds-mo`, `edu-eviction-process-mo`, `periodic-tenancy-notice-mo`, `edu-notice-to-quit-mo`, `criminal-activity-mo` or `edu-expedited-drug-eviction-mo` contradicts `edu-cares-act-notice`: each states Missouri law only, and the CARES row is framed as a federal notice 'on top of your state's notices' (FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 9058(c) read). The new `edu-rent-demand-bar-mo` points to it. Two rows say flatly that no notice is needed (`edu-nonpayment-demand-mo`, `edu-expedited-drug-eviction-mo`); an optional pointer sentence is proposed in §6.
- Item 7, `no-alterations`: confirmed. Its last sentence ('does not limit any repair, installation, rekeying, or reasonable modification that applicable law entitles Tenant to perform') preserves the reasonable-modification right, which lets the landlord condition permission 'on the renter's agreeing to restore the interior of the premises' (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 213.040(2)(1)), and the federal antenna rule, which bars a 'lease provision' that impairs a qualifying antenna on property within the tenant's exclusive use (FEDERAL: 47 C.F.R. § 1.4000(a)(1)). Batch 4 found no Missouri statute giving a tenant a right to install telecom wiring, solar equipment or an EV charger (`edu-telecom-access-mo`, `edu-portable-solar-mo`, `edu-ev-charging-requirements-mo`; G144, G150, G151). Mo. Rev. Stat. § 442.404 binds homeowners' associations, not landlords (`edu-hoa-mo`).
- Item 6, `hoa-compliance`: no conflict (batch-4 finding in §6): fines are assessed against the unit owner after notice and a hearing (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 448.3-102(1)(11)), so passing a tenant-caused fine through is a contract allocation; it is billed, not withheld from the deposit (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.300(4)), as `edu-hoa-mo` says.

**batch-1**
- Failed-positive batteries, each rerun under a new id; no row cites the failed run: G19 (441.610 positive failed on a narrow tenancy limb) rerun as G20; G27 (482.340 positive failed on the section limb) rerun as G28; G37 (571.510 positive failed: "shall be liable in tort" wording) rerun as G38; G43 (610.143 positive failed on the tenant section limb) rerun as G44; G50 (synthetic positive failed the pattern) rerun as G51; G53 (synthetic positive lacked a context word) rerun as G54.
- exculpatory-clauses: G38 missed Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.040(2) ("neither the landlord nor ... shall be liable"), found while reading for eviction-penalty-clause-ban; widened as G44 (shall be liable / no liability) and G46 (without liability), which added Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.060(6), Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.065 and Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.040(2); the row was revised before the batch closed.
- habitability-waiver / habitability-modifiable / habitability-materiality: answered Present (statutory anti-waiver rules and remedy thresholds exist) with a CONFIRMED ABSENT segment for a general habitability statute; the implied warranty of habitability is case law, not read; each row states the cautious course (Part 5).
- binding-arbitration (GAPS item 9): arbitration-agreement NOT tagged MO; edu-binding-arbitration-mo says the library doesn't offer it and why. (1) Whether the law allows it: Chapter 435 read whole (27 sections); Mo. Rev. Stat. § 435.350 validates pre-dispute clauses "except contracts of insurance and contracts of adhesion"; Mo. Rev. Stat. § 435.465 reaches contracts between commercial persons and those they contract with; Mo. Rev. Stat. § 435.460 requires the ten-point capitals notice by the signatures; Mo. Rev. Stat. § 435.375 bars advance waiver of counsel (the clause keeps a lawyer). Whether a standard residential lease is a contract of adhesion, and whether the FAA (FEDERAL: 9 U.S.C. § 2) governs a given lease and displaces the adhesion exclusion and the notice (the revisor's annotation to Mo. Rev. Stat. § 435.460 reports FAA preemption, annotation only), are case law. The clause's carve-outs would fit: possession cases before associate circuit judges (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 534.060, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.020, which also lets other lease sums be joined), small claims to $5,000 (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 482.305). (2) Whether a penalty attaches: no statute ties a remedy to including or enforcing a voided term (G30 0 hits; G31 read: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.234, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.610, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 571.510 void or bar only; Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.300(6) only wrongful withholding; Mo. Rev. Stat. § 442.920(5) civil penalty is for violating the sale-leaseback act). But the Merchandising Practices Act reaches leases of real estate (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 407.010(4), (6); Mo. Rev. Stat. § 407.020(1); private action with punitive damages and fees, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 407.025(1)-(2)), and whether an unenforceable lease term is an "unfair practice" rests on 15 CSR 60 (not in corpus) and case law. Under Taylor's rule (decision 2026-11-07) an answer that turns on case law means no clause; the verdict does not change whatever 15 CSR 60 says (a rule saying yes would only add a penalty). Row VERIFIED on that basis; 15 CSR 60 requested for the knowing-use row.
- part5-nonwaivable: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.233 (lockout/utility) and Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.920 (DV) carry no waiver words; the row tells landlords to treat them as beyond the lease (cautious course) rather than stating they are non-waivable.
- Rule 54 options found and declined (each has an education row saying lawful-or-unsettled and not offered): pre-dispute jury waiver (edu-jury-waiver-mo; no statute on lease waivers, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 510.190(2) lists the ways to waive); confession of judgment (edu-confession-of-judgment-mo; Mo. Rev. Stat. § 511.070 to Mo. Rev. Stat. § 511.100 require a sworn written statement, no lease rule); homestead and exemption waiver (edu-homestead-waiver-mo; no Chapter 513 waiver section); smoke-drift claim waiver (edu-smoke-drift-waiver-mo); binding arbitration (above); special covenant to rebuild after fire under Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.010 (edu-maintenance-duty-shift-mo; same section bars suits over accidental fires, reach unsettled); notice-to-quit waiver under Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.070 (edu-notice-to-quit-waiver-mo; Taylor's 2026-09-30 decline restated under its own key).
- Rule 54 options offered: showing-notice-la tagged (no Missouri entry statute); landlord-self-cure-as tagged (contract right; cost not Rent fits Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.020); tenant-repair-agreement-hi tagged (no Missouri formality rule); new clause promised-repairs-mo (structure from promised-repairs-ut; SERVES_LANDLORD on the Merchandising Practices Act's "false promise" bar, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 407.020(1)).
- Tags not made: arbitration-agreement (above); promised-repairs-wy and promised-repairs-ut (each rests on its own state's statute; Missouri got its own row); the maintenance-duty-shift clauses on the topic are all single-state and rest on their own statutes; Missouri is already tagged on tenant-maintenance, snow-removal and landscaping-irrigation.
- smoke-drift-waiver: whether an apartment building's enclosed common hallway is a "public place" under the Indoor Clean Air Act (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 191.765(5): enclosed indoor area "used by the general public or serving as a place of work", including corridors and elevators; private residences excluded, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 191.769(6)) is unsettled; row gives the cautious course (ban smoking in enclosed common areas, which smoking-policy-mo already does).
- homestead-waiver: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 513.475 and Mo. Rev. Stat. § 513.430 each quoted in the version in force (2026-11-09) and the version effective January 1, 2027 (2026 H.B. 1870 merged with S.B. 835 and S.B. 1111): homestead $15,000 -> $40,000; household goods $3,000 -> $15,000.

**batch-2**
- dv-confidentiality: Absent row (edu-no-dv-confidentiality-rule-mo). Safe at Home (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 589.660 to Mo. Rev. Stat. § 589.683) binds agencies and courts only; the cautious course (separate file, no sharing) is drafting judgment.
- dv-deposit-timing: battery G13 (section-wide AND, deposit + DV + tenancy word) recorded as failed because I gave it a wrong real positive (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 455.050 has no deposit word); rerun as G14 with a synthetic positive only (no section holds all three term sets, so no real positive exists). Not cited in any row.
- dv-deposit-timing: the advice not to take the Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.920(6) termination fee from the deposit is a cautious reading of Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.300(4)'s closed list; the co-tenant split is drafting judgment.
- dv-eviction-protection: Present row (edu-dv-eviction-protection-mo); no bifurcation statute (G15), so removing only the abuser needs agreement, a court order or an ordinary eviction.
- dv-lockchange: Absent row (edu-no-dv-lockchange-mo). The body warns that a landlord-side lock change against a co-tenant abuser without a court order risks forcible entry and detainer under Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.233(1). G17 (lock-change phrases with tenancy word, 0 hits) is NOT cited: its pattern could not match "changes the locks" (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 534.602), so I relied on G16 and G18 instead.
- dv-protection-order-chapter-moved: Present row (edu-dv-law-citations-mo); rule 77 scan script over operative text for references to Chapter 455 and 589 sections not in the corpus.
- dv-qualifying-documents: Present row; Missouri has one list (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.920(4)) and no other landlord-facing DV protection with its own list.
- law-enforcement-cooperation: battery G24 recorded as failed (its real positive, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.740, lacks the pattern's words, and the nuisance limb was too broad); rerun as G25 without the nuisance limb and with Mo. Rev. Stat. § 374.352 as the real positive. Not cited. The row states any-person duties found by G26 (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 58.451, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 58.720, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 194.117, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 192.2405(1)(1), Mo. Rev. Stat. § 630.163(1)).
- entity-landlord-court-representation: battery G32 (fictitious name near suit words) recorded as failed because its real positive, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 417.210, has no suit word within 1,200 characters; rerun as G33 with Mo. Rev. Stat. § 351.584 as the real positive. G32 not cited. The row also covers the FL-gaps 3 items for this topic: foreign LLC, corporation and limited partnership suit bars (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 347.163(1), Mo. Rev. Stat. § 351.574(1), Mo. Rev. Stat. § 359.551(1)), fictitious names (no suit bar), and long-arm service (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 506.500(1)(4)).
- entity-landlord-court-representation: the body tells an out-of-state entity to register before renting, because whether owning and leasing Missouri homes is "transacting business" is case law (not read) and the statutory safe-harbour lists don't mention it.
- eviction-hardship-stay: Present row; the only statutory stays are tender (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.160), appeal bond (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.110, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 534.380) and the drug-eviction stays (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.770(4), Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.880); Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.012 bars local eviction moratoria. Court-rule stays not searched (said in the row).
- eviction-service-party (rule 54 decision): a lease clause in which each tenant appoints co-tenants as agent to receive service of process is a possible lease-invoked option under Mo. Rev. Stat. § 506.150(1)(1) ('an agent authorized by appointment'), but whether a pre-dispute appointment in a form lease satisfies the statute is case law and court-rule territory not read; DECLINED, and the education row (edu-eviction-service-party-mo) says so and tells a landlord who wants it to take advice.
- possession-bond: battery G59 recorded as failed (its section limb had only landlord/tenant words, so the appeal-bond sections Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.110 and Mo. Rev. Stat. § 534.380, which say "defendant", failed); rerun as G60 with a rent word added. G59 not cited.
- rent-into-court-counterclaim: battery G63 recorded as failed for the same section-limb reason (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.110); rerun as G64. G63 not cited.
- foreign-ownership: Present row; Missouri's alien agricultural-land law (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 442.560 to Mo. Rev. Stat. § 442.592) reaches a residential lease only where the leased land is a more-than-five-acre farmable tract and the lease runs ten years or more counting options (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 442.581); no foreign-adversary statute (G36).
- tenant-bankruptcy: battery G72 recorded as failed (wrongly chosen real positive Mo. Rev. Stat. § 515.585, which has no bankruptcy word); rerun as G73 with Mo. Rev. Stat. § 82.462. G72 not cited. Exemption figures quoted from both printed versions of Mo. Rev. Stat. § 513.430 and Mo. Rev. Stat. § 513.475 (in force until 1/1/2027; 2027 versions from 2026 H.B. 1870 merged with S.B. 835 & 1111).
- confirmed-absences-outside-title: battery G75 recorded as failed (real positive Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.300 failed the context limb: its carpet-cleaning receipt sentence has no payment word within 300 characters); rerun as G76 with Mo. Rev. Stat. § 408.130. G75 not cited.
- confirmed-absences-habitability, -misc, -outside-title: written as summary rows (status Present) that point to existing VERIFIED MO rows for absences recorded in the 2026-09-30 pass, plus this batch's own batteries (G40, G41, G76, G77 and the batch's topic batteries). They don't re-audit the existing rows; each existing row keeps its own basis.
- statutory-early-termination: Present row naming Missouri's three statutory exits (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 41.944, the Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.920(3) rent defense, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.645 casualty) and FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955 (saved, read whole); no other exit (G45). infirmity-termination: Absent row (G47, G49).
- rent-demand-bar (item 8(d)): Present row (receivership bars, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.580 and Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.650(9)); no substandard-unit bar (G52). Points to edu-cares-act-notice. 8(d) check for this topic: edu-nonpayment-demand-mo states Missouri law only (no notice period before a rent-and-possession filing) and does not contradict the federal 30-day notice; the row tells landlords to give the federal notice for covered properties. I did not check the other 8(d) rows (edu-cure-grounds-mo, edu-eviction-process-mo, periodic-tenancy-notice-mo, edu-notice-to-quit-mo, criminal-activity-mo, edu-expedited-drug-eviction-mo); that remains for the main pass.
- minor-tenant-filing: Present row (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 431.055, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 431.056, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 507.190); no filing bar (G67).
- limitation-of-actions: Present row; the body tells landlords to treat five years (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 516.120(1)) as the deadline for lease rent because whether a lease is a writing for the payment of money (ten years, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 516.110(1)) is case law, not read.
- landlord-remedies-termination: Present row; no acceleration statute (G70); the effect of Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.170's discharge on later rent is case law, so the body says to claim accrued rent and mitigated losses only.
- social-security-defense: Absent row (G74). rent-into-court-counterclaim: Absent row (G64). possession-bond: Present row (appeal bonds only; G60).

**batch-3**
- deposit-escheat: treated every landlord as a holder under Mo. Rev. Stat. § 447.535 (the act does not define "ordinary course of the holder's business" for a small individual landlord); cautious course.
- nonrefundable-deposit-notice: declined to tag nonrefundable-fees-wv; in Missouri a listed nonrefundable fee is lawful only if it is not security (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.300(8)), and that condition belongs in the bracket (rule 26 [NV-gaps 1]); wrote nonrefundable-fees-mo on the NC model instead.
- nonrefundable-fees-mo keeps cleaning and damage charges out of the nonrefundable list (cautious course; whether an upfront cleaning fee is security is case law, not read).
- fee-in-lieu-of-deposit: insurance-licensing question for landlords who sell deposit-alternative products not researched (insurance chapters, CSR); the row tells the landlord to check.
- required-fees: declined to tag fees-and-charges-vi; its bracket takes any fee and amount, but a Missouri returned-check charge is capped (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 570.120(6)(2)) and a nonrefundable charge must not be security (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.300(8)), so the limits go in the bracket (rule 26 [NV-gaps 1]); wrote fees-and-charges-mo. Its month-to-month sentence (new fee by written notice effective on a rent date at least one month after receipt) borrows Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.060(4)(1); drafting judgment, no statute sets a notice for a new fee.
- collection-fee: offered collection-fee-mo (actual agency charge under a landlord-stated cap, not rent, not withheld from the deposit); no Missouri statute bars or authorizes it (G103); enforceability of cost-shifting is contract case law, not read; FDCPA not read.
- notice-service-fee: no clause offered. Missouri requires no served notice before a rent-and-possession case (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.020, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.140), so a notice fee pays for a step the law does not require, and a fixed fee turns on the penalty doctrine (case law); consistent with batch-1's edu-no-eviction-penalty-rule-mo. The education row says an actual-cost term is a landlord's own choice, with advice.
- statutory-caps: battery G122 recorded as failed (its real positive Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.080 says "tenant" and "person entitled", not "landlord"), rerun as G123 with the role limb widened; the row cites G123 only (check.py rejects any mention of a failed battery, so the "recorded as failed, rerun as" wording lives here and in the record's log line).
- late-fee-limit: the usury statutes (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 408.020, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 408.030, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 408.050, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 408.095) do not name late fees; whether a flat residential late fee is "interest" is case law, not read; the row gives the cautious course and keeps edu-no-late-fee-cap-mo unchanged.
- lockout-for-rent-delinquency: battery G142 recorded as failed (its synthetic positive said "door locks", which the pattern did not allow), rerun as G143 with "door/entry locks" added; the row cites G143 only.
- utility-deposit-return: battery G145 recorded as failed (real positive Mo. Rev. Stat. § 447.517 has no tenancy word, so the tenancy limb rejected it), rerun as G146 without the limb; the row cites G146 and companion G147 only.
- utility-deposit-return: the body tells landlords not to deduct unpaid utility charges from the deposit without the tenant's written agreement, because Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.300(4)(1) allows withholding only for "rent", Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.005(5) defines rent as a stated payment for possession, and Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.020 says other lease sums are not rent "for purposes of this chapter" (chapter 535, which holds the deposit section). This is a reading, not settled law (case law not read); cautious course.
- governmental-fines: tagged tenant-caused-fines-as (no Missouri bar or default rule, G128). alarm-tampering-fee: tagged detector-care-la (actual cost only) and wrote edu-no-alarm-tampering-fee-mo declining a fixed tampering fee (penalty doctrine is case law).
- government-fee-reimbursement: wrote government-fee-reimbursement-mo on the North Dakota model (tenant-caused government fees at actual cost; landlord's own license, registration and inspection fees excluded), so the clause does not depend on any local ordinance. Passing a landlord's own local rental-license fee through to the tenant is not offered: those fees and any pass-through limits are set by ordinance (rule 3); the education row tells the landlord to check local rules.
- carpet-cleaning: declined to tag carpet-cleaning-ar; it applies carpet cleaning to the deposit "as damages caused by Tenant's noncompliance with this Lease", a ground outside the closed list of Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.300(4), and lacks the statutory notice and receipt; deposit-carpet-cleaning-mo (topic deposit-cost-schedule) is Missouri's clause, pointed to from edu-carpet-cleaning-mo.

**batch-4**
- health-district-rental-rules: Absent row (edu-no-health-district-rental-rules-mo). County health rule power (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 192.300) and the Boone County habitability option (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 64.207) are stated as what local rules can reach; no preemption reaches local health codes (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.043 lists five ordinance types only).
- lead-safe-certification: Absent row (edu-no-lead-safe-certification-mo). Battery G91 recorded as failed (its named real positive, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 701.312, says license, not certificate); rerun as G93 with synthetic positives only. G91 not cited. Local lead-safe certificate ordinances are expressly allowed (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 701.324), so no preemption.
- good-cause-notice: Absent row (edu-no-good-cause-notice-mo). G101 recorded as failed (named real positive Mo. Rev. Stat. § 448.4-112 has no cause word), rerun as G102; G107 recorded as failed (named real positive Mo. Rev. Stat. § 534.602 lacks the notice limb), rerun as G110. A section-wide companion with three lookahead limbs was stopped unrun (too slow) and is not logged. Whether a charter city may adopt a just-cause rule is left to case law; no statute preempts it.
- term-change-notice: education row edu-term-change-notice-mo plus optional clause term-change-notice-mo (rule 54). The clause makes the change notice also a one-month termination notice and an offer of a new tenancy, so it works whether or not Missouri courts enforce notice-only term changes (case law, not read).
- lease-registration: Present row (edu-lease-registration-mo). The recording act (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 442.380 to Mo. Rev. Stat. § 442.400) reaches leases through the chattels-real definition (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 442.010(3)); whether possession gives a later buyer notice is case law, so the body gives the cautious course (record long leases and options).
- landlord-registration: Present row (edu-landlord-registration-mo), rule_type CONDITIONAL. No statewide registration; St. Louis City nonresident-owner agent (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 82.817), statewide nonresident/corporate landlord agent (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.520(4)(2)), vacant-building registration fee (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 67.399), Boone County bar (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 64.207(4)(3)). No statute preempts other local rental registration or licensing.
- single-family-zone-lease-limit and accessory-dwelling-unit: Absent rows; zoning is local (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 89.020, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 64.090); Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.043 does not reach zoning; whether zoning may distinguish rented from owner-occupied homes is case law. Tagged accessory-dwelling-unit-term-co (contract-based; basis checked both ways).
- rights-responsibilities-guide: Absent row (edu-no-rights-guide-mo); only Mo. Rev. Stat. § 701.305 (health department lead web information, no landlord delivery duty).
- hoa: Present row (edu-hoa-mo) plus tag of association-approval-la. The body warns not to deduct association fines from the deposit, because Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.300(4) lists only three withholding grounds; whether a leasing restriction is a use restriction needing unanimous consent (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 448.2-117(4)) is left to case law.
- association-obligations-disclosure: Absent row for ordinary leases, with the twenty-year-lease resale-certificate rule (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 448.1-103(24), Mo. Rev. Stat. § 448.4-109); tag of association-documents-delivery-ks (contract-based; its agency sentence is not an exculpation).
- tenant-display-rights: Present education row plus optional clause display-rules-mo (rule 54). Whether Mo. Rev. Stat. § 442.404(2)(1) (covenants 'running with the land') reaches a lease is unsettled; the clause allows a political yard sign in association communities subject to reasonable rules (cautious course). Single-state display clauses (tenant-display-rules-co, display-and-signage-rules-wy, tenant-display-rights-nd, us-flag-display-la/-ar, flag-display-nv) not tagged: each cites or rests on its own state's statute.
- ev-charging-requirements: Present row (edu-ev-charging-requirements-mo) plus tag of ev-charger-installation-hi; ev-charger-installation-conditions-nd not tagged (rule 6, one curated installation clause). ev-charging-shared-area: tag of ev-charging-shared-area-co only (no MO statute; G144). ev-charging-end-of-tenancy: Absent education row; ev-charging-end-of-tenancy-co declined because its 'remains Tenant property' sentence conflicts with the hi clause's leave-in-place-as-Landlord-property choice.
- Weights and measures (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 413.226(2)) reaches electrical charging stations; whether a landlord billing tenants by use is a retail location is unsettled and the CSR weights-and-measures rules were not searched; the row says to check with the division or use a flat charge.
- Item 5 (local limits): Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.043(2)(1) reaches only an ordinance that bars refusing to rent because the renter's lawful source of income 'includes funding from a federal or other housing assistance program'; on its text it binds 'county or city, or county or city with a charter form of government' (towns and villages are not named) and it does not reach health codes, zoning, rental registration or licensing, lead rules, tenant notices, term-change rules or just-cause rules. An ordinance protecting other lawful income (not housing assistance) is outside (2)(1), but (2)(2) separately bars restricting a landlord's 'income-qualifying methods'. Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.012 bars only eviction moratoria. Mo. Rev. Stat. § 64.207 (Boone), Mo. Rev. Stat. § 67.452 (Jackson County nuisance actions) and Mo. Rev. Stat. § 82.1025 (St. Louis City, Kansas City, Springfield, St. Charles nuisance actions) are authorizations by population bracket, not preemptions of the batch's topics. Charter powers: Mo. Const. art. VI, § 19(a) (cities), Mo. Const. art. VI, § 18(c) (counties). No Kansas City, St. Louis or Boone County ordinance was met in the text read.
- telecom-access: Present row (edu-telecom-access-mo) using FEDERAL: 47 C.F.R. § 1.4000 (saved by the main pass mid-batch; paragraphs (a) and (b) read) and Mo. Rev. Stat. § 392.460 (owner notices to occupants when the local phone carrier is relieved); tag of telecom-access-mn (its saving paragraph confines the consent sentence to what the federal rule allows). telecom-access-wy not tagged (cites Wyoming sections).
- portable-solar: Present row plus optional clause portable-solar-mo (rule 54), whose utility-approval condition tracks Mo. Rev. Stat. § 386.890(15). Single-state solar clauses (va, vt, co, wy, nj, ut) not tagged: each rests on or cites its own state's law.
- political-access, religious-cultural-display, tenant-right-to-organize: Absent rows. G136 recorded as failed (named real positive Mo. Rev. Stat. § 442.404 says person seeking elected office), rerun as G137; G133 recorded as failed (named real positive Mo. Rev. Stat. § 213.040 has no display word near religion), rerun as G134. Neither failed run is cited as support.

**batch-5**
- utility-allowance-cap: tagged utility-allowance-cap-co (no MO cap; overage not rent under Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.020) and wrote edu-utility-allowance-cap-mo for the housing trust fund allowance (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 215.037) and the regulated-utility doubt (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 386.020(15)); the doubt concerns metered resale at a markup, which the clause does not do, so it is a cautious course, not a condition of lawfulness.
- utility-disconnection-notice-authorization: Present for municipal/district water and sewer (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 250.140(2) sends notices to the owner by law); PSC-regulated utilities Not located (CSR); row NEEDS_REVIEW with needs_source. The CSR number was kept out of the notes because the checker errors on a CSR citation not saved in work/sources; it is in needs_source and SOURCE REQUESTS.
- utility-transfer: tagged utility-transfer-nj (its no-shutoff sentence matches Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.233(2), reimbursement not Rent per Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.020) and wrote edu-no-utility-transfer-cutoff-mo (PROHIBITED); a Tennessee-style cutoff clause is not offered because a lease cannot supply a cutoff ground Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.233(2) lacks.
- electric-submetering-disclosure: Present on the Mo. Rev. Stat. § 386.020(15) definitions (exclusion only for distribution 'not for sale to others'), cautious course no resale; NEEDS_REVIEW for the Commission metering rules.
- Lead for the home-business topic (not in this batch): Mo. Rev. Stat. § 71.990(2) lets a resident run a home-based business unless restricted by 'Any deed restriction, covenant, or agreement restricting the use of land' (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 71.990(2)(1)); whether a lease is such an agreement is for that topic's helper. Cited here only in the sprinkler rows (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 71.990(5)(3)).
- Rows written NEEDS_REVIEW: only edu-utility-disconnection-notice-authorization-mo and edu-electric-submetering-disclosure-mo (both wait on Public Service Commission CSR rules; bodies state nothing about the rules' content). Every other row in the batch is VERIFIED on statutes read whole; where a point turns on unread case law (water/sewer lien vs. the 90-day owner cap; tenement house in the fire-escape law; consent to a utility cutoff; whether tenants are 'the public' for a landlord well; lodging-establishment reach) the row states the doubt and the cautious course (Part 5).
- Tags (5 in all): utility-allowance-cap-co, utility-transfer-nj, energy-data-release-wy, appliances-excluded-la, pest-cooperation-pr; each with a both-ways basis check and fee screen in its MO segment. Declined: a Tennessee-style utility cutoff clause (utility-transfer-tn) because Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.233(2) bars the interruption; pest-cooperation-la not used since pest-cooperation-pr carries household members, matching the persons in Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.234(3).
- heating: edu-no-heat-standard-mo also states the Boiler and Pressure Vessel Act duty (biennial certificate for low-pressure heating and hot water supply boilers in apartment houses of six or more units, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 650.230(2), Mo. Rev. Stat. § 650.260(2)(2), misdemeanor Mo. Rev. Stat. § 650.270, not in cities or charter counties regulating by ordinance Mo. Rev. Stat. § 650.290), the library's usual home for boiler rules being heating, water-heater-temperature and steam-radiator-covers; the other two rows point to it.
- fire-code-standard: Present on the statewide fire-escape law (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 320.010 to Mo. Rev. Stat. § 320.060: owner or lessee of every hotel, boarding and lodging house and tenement house of three or more stories; misdemeanor, each day separate). The statute does not define tenement house; the row tells landlords to treat any apartment building of three or more stories as covered. Lodging-establishment licensing (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 315.005 to Mo. Rev. Stat. § 315.065, five or more guest rooms held out to the public, including an apartment hotel) is mentioned in fire-code-standard and pool-safety only as applying to places that rent rooms to the public.
- pool-safety: Present on Mo. Rev. Stat. § 577.161 (life jackets at pools of apartments and condominiums with five or more living units), Mo. Rev. Stat. § 537.348(3) (recreational-use act does not limit liability for pools or residential areas), Ethan's Law (pay-for-entry pools only) and lodging pools; no barrier or notice duty (G204).
- asbestos-hazards and private-well-testing: Present (CONDITIONAL) on the abatement program (scope follows 40 CFR Part 61 Subpart M as adopted by state rule, not read; row says ask the department) and on the public water system definition (15 connections or 25 people, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 640.102(6)) with testing, reporting and noncompliance notice (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 640.120, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 640.125) plus well plugging (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 256.615).
- Elevator safety (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 701.350 to Mo. Rev. Stat. § 701.383; Mo. Rev. Stat. § 701.371 read: no owner or lessee may operate an unregistered installation) surfaced in G215 but no topic in this batch is about elevators; no row written. See PROPOSED TOPIC QUESTIONS.

**batch-6**
- required-disclosures / statutory-forms: the sale-leaseback disclosure (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 442.920(3)) is verbatim statutory text, but it must reach the seller at least fourteen calendar days before the sale leaseback agreement is executed and on its own single page, so the lease cannot be its vehicle; answered as education (edu-required-disclosures-mo, edu-statutory-forms-mo), no clause.
- actual-notice-method: offered optional clause electronic-notice-agreement-mo (CONSTRAINED_TERM | SERVES_LANDLORD). The UETA reaches a writing requirement only between parties who agreed to deal electronically (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 432.220(2)), so the agreement must sit in the lease; termination, possession, abandonment, rent-demand and court notices and the deposit statement are carved out as the cautious course (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 432.235(2); Mo. Rev. Stat. § 1.190 reach to email unsettled in the text read); the non-waivable withdrawal right (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 432.220(3)) is kept in the clause.
- adverse-proceeding-notice: did not tag tenant-notice-of-adverse-proceeding-ks; its notes rest on K.S.A. 58-2514, and its no-attornment sentence omits the sale-under-execution or deed-of-trust and forfeited-mortgage exceptions of Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.150(2)-(3) and the successor-in-title route of Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.081. MO already carries tenant-forward-proceedings-ca for the forwarding duty, so no new MO clause.
- Absence rows for topics named after another state's statute (military-air-zone, ordnance-demolition-meter, shoreline, prop65, tpa-notice, tpa-exemption-notice, tpa-sunset, repair-escrow-exemption-notice) each rest on a Missouri battery on the substance plus an everyday-word companion where the first battery had only synthetic positives (G229/G230, G233/G234, G235, G236, G242, G243, G244, G248).
- inspection-condemnation-disclosure, foreclosure-disclosure, defective-drywall-disclosure, military-air-zone-disclosure: no statutory disclosure duty, but each body gives the cautious course (disclose a known material fact) on the Merchandising Practices Act concealment rule (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 407.020(1)); materiality in a lease is case law, not read.
- written-notice-required answered as one education row listing the written notices in Chapters 441, 534 and 535 (script over every section for writing words); no clause, since the shared notices clause (tagged MO) already defers to statutory methods.

**batch-7**
- automatic-renewal: wrote MO's own optional clause automatic-renewal-mo (SERVES_LANDLORD) instead of tagging automatic-renewal-ks. The KS clause's single {{renewal_notice_days}} non-renewal notice also governs a month-to-month renewal, so a builder figure under one month would shorten the Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.060(4)(1) notice, which only a special agreement under Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.070 could do (an option the library declines, edu-notice-to-quit-mo); that condition cannot live in notes (rule 26). The MO clause routes a month-to-month renewal to the statutory notice.
- rent-escalation: tagged rent-escalation-la (schedule or index fixed at signing). Declined rent-increase-midterm-ky (unilateral mid-term increase with tenant exit): no Missouri statute bars it, but its enforceability is case law (not read) and it conflicts with term-change-notice-mo (batch-4), under which fixed-term terms change only by written agreement. Rule 54 education given in edu-no-rent-escalation-rule-mo.
- rent-concession, rent-installments, tax-escalation: tagged rent-concession-wi, rent-installments-or and tax-insurance-escalation-la as written (contract terms; no Missouri statute; tax share is not rent for possession under Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.020, and the clause does not call it Rent).
- lease-term-limitation: whether Missouri's recording statute (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 442.380, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 442.400) reaches a residential lease is case law, not read; the row states the cautious course (record a memorandum of a long lease).
- guarantor-renewal: tagged guaranty-as. Missouri's surety-demand statute (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 433.010 to Mo. Rev. Stat. § 433.030: a surety 'in any bond, bill or note' may demand suit and is exonerated if suit is not begun within thirty days) may or may not reach a lease guaranty (case law, not read); the clause waives nothing, and edu-guarantor-renewal-mo gives the cautious course (sue within 30 days of such a demand).
- owner-move-in-reservation, extended-absence-notice, periodic-services-entry, key-control-policy, tenant-portal, emergency-contact: tagged owner-occupancy-termination-ar, extended-absence-notice-ks, scheduled-services-entry-la, lock-change-ak, tenant-portal-mn and emergency-contact-co as contract terms (no Missouri statute on any of them). lock-change-ak's five-day key handover is a carried drafting figure, not a Missouri figure (said in the segment).
- rules-regulations: tagged rules-wi; declined rules-ia (unilateral mid-term rule changes on notice), for the same reason as rent-increase-midterm-ky: case law not read, and it conflicts with term-change-notice-mo. Rule 54 education in edu-no-house-rules-statute-mo.
- home-business: tagged home-business-permission-co beside residential-use-only; whether a lease is an 'agreement restricting the use of land' under Mo. Rev. Stat. § 71.990(2)(1) is case law, so the rows say to state the rule in the lease.
- parking-rules-notice: did not tag parking-rules-notice-co (its rule_type REQUIRED and receipt-before-signing structure come from Colorado's statute; Missouri has no such duty); education row only, pointing to parking-vehicle-rules and the towing sign rule.
- Batteries recorded as failed and rerun: G249 -> G250, G255 -> G257, G270 -> G271, G274 and G276 -> G277 (each row cites only the passing rerun or the failed form).

**batch-8**
- animal-care-access: tagged animal-care-access-vi as written (tenant advance consent; Missouri has no entry statute) and added edu-no-animal-care-entry-right-mo; the education row warns that a landlord who feeds or shelters an animal three or more consecutive days harbors it and becomes an owner with the care duty (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 578.005(7), (9)).
- construction-liens: tagged no-liens-ar (tenant no-liens clause) and added edu-construction-liens-mo; whether a consenting landlord fee is liened for tenant work turns on Mo. Rev. Stat. § 429.150 benefit and agency case law, not read; cautious course stated.
- designated-repairer: no clause offered. Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.234(2) requires only workmanlike work and voids any lease waiver of the remedy; a lease term naming the repairer may be read as a waiver (case law, not read), so the education row gives the cautious course instead (rule 54).
- condemned-premises-rent-bar: no statute; body tells landlords not to charge rent while a government order bars occupancy (cautious course), and points to Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.645 and the receivership row.
- eminent-domain: tagged eminent-domain-vi (award to landlord fits Mo. Rev. Stat. § 523.053(1) advance agreement among defendants; relocation payments carved out, consistent with Mo. Rev. Stat. § 523.205(9), where only an owner may waive them).
- environmental-event-termination: offered new optional clause environmental-event-termination-mo (SERVES_LANDLORD | CONSTRAINED_TERM; model environmental-event-termination-ut, structure only). Fault persons use the tenant-caused-damage-mo test ("deliberate or negligent act or omission of Tenant, an Occupant, or a guest or invitee of Tenant"), matching item 8(c)'s proposal for casualty-termination-mo; if the main pass does not adopt that proposal, the casualty clause and this clause differ in fault persons and one should be aligned. The 10-day landlord-termination floor is a drafting judgment (Missouri's ten-day lease-violation notice), not a statutory figure. Lead hazards are left out of the trigger list because of Mo. Rev. Stat. § 701.308(2).
- prohibited-acts-renter: did not tag prohibited-acts-renter-wy. Its promise not to "negligently ... damage" the property could make a tenant liable for a negligently started fire, which Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.010 may bar ('any fire shall accidentally begin'; reach is case law). Wrote prohibited-acts-renter-mo with the same carve-out tenant-caused-damage-mo uses, plus the statutory waste duty (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.030).
- furnishings-included: tagged furnishings-included-la and added edu-furnishings-included-mo, because whether supplied furniture is part of the "dwelling unit" for deposit withholding (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.300(4)(2)) is unsettled and wrongful withholding doubles; cautious course stated.
- informal-dispute-resolution: tagged informal-dispute-resolution-or (non-binding, excludes possession actions; not arbitration, consistent with edu-binding-arbitration-mo).
- frozen-standard-incorporation: answered Present (edu-code-editions-mo): no statewide code for private rentals (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 320.405(5)(3), 2026 S.B. 1421); fixed editions only in Mo. Rev. Stat. § 319.200 (seismic) and Mo. Rev. Stat. § 67.281(2) (2006 IRC R317). Consistent with edu-fire-code-standard-mo (batch-5).
- portfolio-thresholds: answered Present as a pointer summary; the Missouri Human Rights Act single-family exemption needs both conditions (no more than three houses AND no broker or advertising).
- Battery note: a section-wide AND written as two lookaheads in --secctx ran over ten minutes and was stopped before logging; rerun with a single secctx term as G294. Failed runs disclosed in rows: G303 (rerun as G306), G320 (rerun as G321), G331 (rerun as G333).

**batch-9**
- purpose-limitation: answered Present (edu-purpose-limitation-mo) on Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.020 and Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.040; whether "shall become void" is automatic or at the lessor's option is case law, so the body tells landlords to proceed by notice and a court case. Overlaps edu-cure-grounds-mo (same sections, other topic); kept short and pointed to it (rule 27).
- quiet-possession: Absent; the only covenant statute (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 442.420) reaches fee simple conveyances only. Body assumes an implied covenant as the cautious course (case law not read).
- repair-notice: no new clause. The tagged landlord-maintenance clause already asks for prompt written repair notice; repair-request-procedure-ne, repair-notice-method-ar and habitability-notice-co rest on their own states' notice statutes and were not tagged.
- renters-insurance-rules: Absent row only; the requirement itself is already carried by tagged tenants-property-insurance-ks-oh-ca. No force-placement clause offered (no Missouri rule to build it on; would be an unprompted new charge clause).
- service-animal-denial-penalty: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 209.160 names only Mo. Rev. Stat. § 209.150 places (including "lodging places"), not the housing section Mo. Rev. Stat. § 209.190; row states the doubt and treats denial as potentially criminal (cautious course). The heading of Mo. Rev. Stat. § 209.190 mentions actual damages but the body has no damages sentence; noted as heading only.
- sex-offender-occupancy: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 566.147 (as amended by 2026 H.B. 2273, in force since 2026-08-28, single version) binds the offender; the landlord-facing exposure found is Mo. Rev. Stat. § 575.159 (aiding a noncompliant offender). Body does not tell landlords to evict on that ground alone (case law).
- tenant-confidential-information vs tenant-records: both cite Mo. Rev. Stat. § 407.1500 and Mo. Rev. Stat. § 407.1355. Kept as two rows, one subject each (disclosure of tenant information; ledger and data-security duties). Flag for the cross-slice near-identical-body check: the two bodies share a breach-notice passage by design.
- tenant-employee-housing: offered optional clause employee-housing-mo (SERVES_LANDLORD): Mo. Rev. Stat. § 534.030(1) makes "premises ... occupied incident to the terms of employment" an unlawful detainer trigger, so stating it is an identification the statute makes a trigger (rule 54). Model employee-housing-ar for structure only; not tagged (Arkansas act exclusion).
- trespass-notice: offered optional clause barred-person-covenant-mo (SERVES_LANDLORD), modelled on barred-person-covenant-ne (not tagged: Nebraska basis and Nebraska DV carve-out). Its DV sentence uses Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.920(2) wording without the statutory exceptions, a deliberate landlord concession so the clause never runs against a victim.
- usufructuary-lessor: no clause; whether a life tenant's lease ends with the life estate is case law, so a status clause's effect would rest on unread law. life-tenant-lessor-status-wy not tagged (Wyoming citations in its text).
- waterbed: tagged waterbed-mn as written (charges nothing; wear-and-tear sentence matches Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.300(4)(2); reuses {{flotation_insurance_amount}}); plus edu-no-waterbed-rule-mo.
- subsidy-habitability-proration: body says the cautious reading of "periodic rent" in Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.234(2) is the full contract rent (larger permitted deduction), since nothing settles it.
- Batteries with a failed positive, each rerun and disclosed in the row as "recorded as failed, rerun as": G314 -> G315, G322 -> G324, G326 -> G327, G346 -> G347. Cause each time: a --possec section that did not contain the pattern words (G314, G322, G326) or a synthetic positive that lacked a context-limb word (G346).

### §6 Findings on existing rows

- Item 8(a), PROPOSED ROW `early-termination-mo` (override of `early-termination-ks` for Missouri; Claude Code applies it at sync by removing MO from `early-termination-ks` and adding this row). Why: the shared fee ('one month's Rent ({{monthly_rent}}) or 30% of the remaining Rent due under the Term, whichever is greater') rests only on penalty doctrine, which is case law not read; no Missouri statute governs lease early-termination fees or liquidated damages (G70, batch-2); deposit withholding for notice failures is limited to 'actual damages sustained as a result of the tenant's failure to give adequate notice to terminate the tenancy pursuant to law or the rental agreement; provided that the landlord makes reasonable efforts to mitigate damages' (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.300(4)(3)). Use the flat one-month fee, as `early-termination-ga`, `-pa`, `-al` and `-ut` do. Drafted by the batch-2 helper; the main pass changed one sentence (the helper's "Landlord will regain possession only through a court proceeding" was wrong: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.065 lets a landlord treat premises as abandoned and dispose of property without court).
  - id `early-termination-mo`; group Default & Termination; title Early Termination; states MO; supersedes `early-termination-ks`; rule_type CONDITIONAL; content_type LEASE_CLAUSE; lease_clause_basis SERVES_LANDLORD; topic_key early-termination; verification_status VERIFIED; effective_from/last_checked 2026-11-09.
  - bodyText: "Tenant may terminate this Lease before the end of the Term by providing Landlord at least 30 days' written notice. Tenant will pay Landlord, by the termination date, an early termination fee equal to one month's Rent ({{monthly_rent}}), and remains responsible for Rent and other obligations up to the termination date. If Tenant gives that notice, pays the fee and delivers possession of the property by the termination date, Tenant owes no Rent for any period after the termination date. If Tenant leaves before the end of the Term without using this option, Tenant's liability is governed by Missouri law. This early-termination option and fee apply only if this Lease has a fixed Term; a month-to-month or other periodic tenancy ends on the notice that applicable law and this Lease provide, without an early termination fee. Landlord may terminate this Lease early in accordance with this Lease's Tenant Default and notice provisions, or if Tenant abandons the property as Missouri law defines abandonment, and will regain possession only through a court proceeding or Missouri's abandoned-premises procedure. Nothing in this Section limits any right either party has under applicable law, including a Tenant's right to terminate because of military service under the Servicemembers Civil Relief Act or Missouri law, a Tenant's protections as a victim of domestic violence, sexual assault or stalking (where the termination fee stated in that Section of this Lease, if any, applies instead of this fee), or a Tenant's release from Rent if the residence is destroyed by a disaster Tenant did not cause."
  - notes: "MO: Override of early-termination-ks (MO gaps pass 2026-11-09, Missouri item 8(a)). Flat one-month fee instead of the greater of one month or 30% of remaining Rent: no Missouri statute governs lease early-termination fees (G70); penalty doctrine is case law, not read; deposit withholding is limited to 'actual damages sustained as a result of the tenant's failure to give adequate notice to terminate the tenancy pursuant to law or the rental agreement; provided that the landlord makes reasonable efforts to mitigate damages' (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.300(4)(3)). Abandonment: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.065. Military: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 41.944; FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955(e)(1) ('The lessor may not impose an early termination charge'). DV: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.920(6); dv-termination-fee-mo. Casualty: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.645. Self-help bar: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.233. Read section-open 2026-11-09 from the Missouri corpus (revisor.mo.gov download, currency 2026-11-09)."
- Item 8(c), proposed first sentence for `casualty-termination-mo` (row not edited): "If the property is damaged or destroyed by fire, tornado or other casualty that does not result from the deliberate or negligent act or omission of Tenant, an Occupant, or a guest or invitee of Tenant, and the damage makes the property unfit to live in or substantially impairs Tenant's use of it, either Landlord or Tenant may end this Lease by written notice to the other, effective on the date Tenant moves out." The rest of the row stands. Why: it matches `tenant-caused-damage-mo`'s fault test, so a casualty that is not the tenant side's fault gives both the exit and abatement, and one that is gives neither. Read with Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.010 (no action against a tenant in whose apartment a fire 'shall accidentally begin or take place') and Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.645 (no rent for the rest of the term 'so long as the tenant was not the person who caused the disaster'): the clause's exit is contractual, so excluding negligent casualties from the exit takes nothing those sections give; `tenant-caused-damage-mo` already preserves both. Whether "accidentally" in Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.010 covers a negligent fire is case law, not read; the proposed wording does not depend on it. Change type: MO-only row.
- Item 8(d), optional pointer (no change needed): `edu-nonpayment-demand-mo` and `edu-expedited-drug-eviction-mo` could end with "If your property has a federally backed mortgage or is in a covered federal housing program, see the federal 30-day notice to vacate." Both state Missouri law correctly as they stand.
- Check part A, existing rows (not in the delta): edu-self-help-eviction-mo has the same "to force a tenant out" narrowing of Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.233(2) that part A corrected in edu-confirmed-absences-habitability-mo and edu-no-lockout-for-rent-mo (batch-5 finding gives the wording); edu-county-habitability-rules-mo drops Mo. Rev. Stat. § 64.207(4)(3)'s qualifier on the licensing bar (inspection "on a regular schedule or before offering" a unit for rent, as the part A checker quoted it). Fix at sync so the rows agree.
- Check part C, existing rows: edu-repair-and-deduct-mo has the same Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.234(3) fault-qualifier gap ('deliberate or negligent act or omission' of the tenant, family or a consenting guest) that part C corrected in edu-no-repair-escrow-exemption-mo.
- Round 2 P2, existing row: periodic-tenancy-notice-mo (existing MO clause; term-change-notice-mo follows its wording) has no mobile-home lot exception. For a tenant who 'occupies and has an ownership interest in a mobile home' and rents the lot, a landlord's notice ending a tenancy of less than one year must end it 'not sooner than sixty days from the date the rent payment next becomes due, notwithstanding any written lease provision' (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.060(4)(2)). Proposed addition to its body: "If Tenant occupies and has an ownership interest in a mobile home and this Lease is for the lot on which it sits, Landlord's notice must end the tenancy no sooner than 60 days from the next day Rent is due after Tenant receives the notice." MO-only row.

**batch-1**
- edu-prohibited-lease-terms-mo lists the terms Missouri voids (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.234(2), Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.610, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 571.510(2), Mo. Const. art. XIV, § 2) but not the 2026 sale-leaseback rule: "No provision of this section shall be modified or waived by any agreement. Any portion of an agreement that is executed, modified, or extended after August 28, 2026, that modifies or waives a duty or remedy under this section is void ab initio and unenforceable." (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 442.920(6), 2026 H.B. 2636 merged with S.B. 834 and S.B. 973, single version, in force). Its reach is narrow (a lease back to a homeowner who sold the home to the buyer-landlord). Proposed wording to add to the body's list: "and, in a sale-leaseback of a home, any agreement term that modifies or waives the seller's protections under the sale-leaseback law." Notes addition: the quote above with its citation. (The topic part5-nonwaivable now states it in edu-nonwaivable-rights-mo.)
- edu-consumer-protection-mo cites the Attorney General's rules as "15 Mo. Code Regs. 60"; the gaps brief's citation form is "15 CSR 60". Cosmetic; align at sync if the citation checker requires one form.
- edu-landlord-duties-mo: body says Missouri gives tenants "a defense to rent when a residence is destroyed by a disaster the tenant did not cause"; Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.645 says 'the tenant shall not be liable to the landlord for rent during the remainder of the term of the lease agreement' (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.645). Consistent; no change needed (checked while writing the habitability rows).
- None of the batch's reads contradicts an existing MO row.

**batch-2**
- PROPOSED ROW (prompt item 8(a)), early-termination-mo, override of early-termination-ks for Missouri. Reason: MO is tagged on early-termination-ks, whose fee is "one month's Rent ({{monthly_rent}}) or 30% of the remaining Rent due under the Term, whichever is greater". Missouri has no statute on early-termination fees or liquidated damages for leases (G70 found no acceleration or remaining-rent statute; edu-no-mitigation-statute-mo), so the fee rests only on the penalty doctrine, which is case law not read (MO log section 7). The deposit law lets a landlord keep only 'actual damages sustained as a result of the tenant's failure to give adequate notice to terminate the tenancy pursuant to law or the rental agreement; provided that the landlord makes reasonable efforts to mitigate damages' (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.300(4)(3)). A fee that scales with the remaining term is the cautious-course risk, so use a flat one-month fee as early-termination-ga, early-termination-pa, early-termination-al and early-termination-ut do. Read section-open 2026-11-09 from the Missouri corpus (revisor.mo.gov download, currency 2026-11-09)." The DV sentence keeps dv-termination-fee-mo's statement that it displaces this fee.
- No defect found in edu-dv-protections-mo against Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.920, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 455.010 and Mo. Rev. Stat. § 455.045 (now read whole); its notes call Mo. Rev. Stat. § 455.010 "not read" and Mo. Rev. Stat. § 455.045 "read as an excerpt", which this batch's rows supersede in substance (edu-dv-law-citations-mo). No change needed.
- Legal-watch items (statute cross-references, no MO row affected; rule 77): Mo. Rev. Stat. § 452.376 cites "sections 455.010 and 455.501" (no Mo. Rev. Stat. § 455.501 in the corpus); Mo. Rev. Stat. § 455.210 and Mo. Rev. Stat. § 455.215 cite "section 455.205" (not in the corpus; revisor asterisk); Mo. Rev. Stat. § 359.551(6) cites "section 351.570" (not in the corpus; the certificate-of-authority rule is Mo. Rev. Stat. § 351.572); Mo. Rev. Stat. § 455.050(1), as amended in 2026, names "cyberstalking", which Mo. Rev. Stat. § 455.010 does not define (the cyberstalking offense, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 565.405, is held as not in force until July 1, 2027); the 2027 version of Mo. Rev. Stat. § 565.225 drops the address-confidentiality-program subdivision of first-degree stalking.

**batch-3**
- None. The rows item 4 lists were read where a new row points to them; none conflicts with the statutes read in this batch.

**batch-4**
- Item 6 hoa-compliance (MO tagged): no conflict. Association fines run against the unit owner after notice and an opportunity to be heard (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 448.3-102(1)(11); lien on the unit, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 448.3-116(1)), so the clause's pass-through of fines Tenant causes is a contract allocation and lawful. Caution, not a defect: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.300(4) lets a landlord withhold from the deposit 'only such amounts as are reasonably necessary' for unpaid rent, restoration and notice damages, so a passed-through fine should be billed, not deducted; edu-hoa-mo says so. No wording change proposed.
- edu-no-ev-charging-rule-mo (ev-charging) says Missouri 'sets no rule for landlords' on EV charging. Still true for tenant installation rights, but Mo. Rev. Stat. § 386.020(15) (charging-station operators are not electrical corporations), Mo. Rev. Stat. § 413.226(2) (weights and measures law applies to electrical charging stations) and Mo. Rev. Stat. § 67.288 (no local EV mandate on church or nonprofit lots) are owner-side rules now stated in edu-ev-charging-requirements-mo. Suggest at sync adding to its body: 'For the rules that apply if you provide or bill for charging, see the EV charging requirements row.' No defect in its stated absence.

**batch-5**
- edu-self-help-eviction-mo narrows the utility-interruption rule. Row: "You may not willfully interrupt essential services such as electricity, gas, water or sewer to force a tenant out, unless you do so for health or safety reasons." Statute has no purpose element: 'Any landlord or its agent who willfully diminishes services to a tenant by interrupting or causing the interruption of essential services, including but not limited to electric, gas, water, or sewer service, to the tenant or to the premises shall be deemed guilty of forcible entry and detainer as described in chapter 534' (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.233(2)). Proposed wording: "You may not willfully interrupt, or cause the interruption of, essential services such as electricity, gas, water or sewer to the tenant or the premises, for any reason other than health or safety."
- Batch-3 draft row edu-utility-deposit-return-mo (not a library row yet) names the Commission utility rules as '4 CSR 240'; the Public Service Commission rules are now cited as 20 CSR 4240 (the batch-5 guidance line uses 20 CSR 4240-13). Suggest the main pass align the boundary statement at assembly.
- edu-water-sewer-owner-liability-mo (topic utilities-responsibility) states owner liability under Mo. Rev. Stat. § 250.140 but not that unpaid sewer charges can become a lien on the land (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 250.234, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 204.455, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 249.645, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 249.255). Not a defect; the lien rule is now stated in edu-municipal-utility-lien-mo (topic municipal-utility-lien), so no change is proposed.

**batch-6**
- edu-notice-service-mo (existing MO row) says "Missouri sets delivery rules for particular notices rather than one general method." Missouri has a general default method: 'Whenever any of the statutes of this state require or imply that a notice shall be given to any person concerning or affecting any right, property, claim, duty, matter or thing of any character or nature, unless the statutes expressly direct a different method of service, the delivery of a true copy of the notice to the person intended to be notified, or the leaving of a copy at his usual place of abode with some member of his family over the age of fifteen years, constitutes a valid and sufficient service of the notice.' (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 1.190; RSMo 1939 § 1437, A.L. 1957 p. 587; single version; read section-open 2026-11-09). It governs the written termination notices of Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.050 and Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.060(4), which name no method (the revisor's cross-reference under Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.050 points to it). Proposed replacement for the first sentence: "Missouri sets delivery rules for some notices; where a statute requires a notice but names no method (for example a notice ending a month-to-month tenancy), delivering a copy to the person, or leaving a copy at the person's home with a family member over 15, is valid service." and add Mo. Rev. Stat. § 1.190 to its notes. The new rows edu-written-notices-mo and edu-actual-notice-method-mo state the rule.

**batch-7**
- residential-use-only (MO tagged): its MO segment does not mention Missouri's home-based business statute, under which 'Any person who resides in a residential dwelling may use the residential dwelling for a home-based business unless such use is restricted by' 'Any deed restriction, covenant, or agreement restricting the use of land' (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 71.990(2), (2)(1); 2022 H.B. 1662; read section-open 2026-11-09). The clause's bar on "any commercial purpose" is the lease restriction that keeps the default from applying, if a lease counts as such an agreement (case law, not read). Proposed addition to its MO segment: "A tenant's home-based business is allowed by default unless an agreement restricting the use of land restricts it (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 71.990(2)(1)); this clause is that restriction. To allow one, use home-business-permission-co (edu-home-business-mo)." No text change needed.
- edu-notice-to-quit-mo says a tenancy at will, by sufferance or for less than a year "may be ended by the landlord on one month's written notice"; Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.060(1) gives the notice to 'the person entitled to the possession'. Same effect for the landlord; the tenant-side notice for a tenancy at will is not stated in the statute (edu-tenancy-at-will-mo states one month as the cautious course). No change needed; noted for consistency.

**batch-8**
- (minor, for the main pass) casualty-termination-mo fault persons ("Tenant, members of Tenant's household or Tenant's guests") differ from the new environmental-event-termination-mo and from tenant-caused-damage-mo; item 8(c)'s proposed first sentence would align all three. No new defect found.

**batch-9**
- edu-source-of-income-mo (topic source-of-income) states that Missouri does not protect source of income but omits Mo. Rev. Stat. § 215.314(6)-(7): for a multifamily project receiving Missouri Housing Development Commission additional subsidies, the owner agreement must assure that 'The owner shall not refuse to accept housing vouchers and section 8 existing housing certificates from tenants in partial payment of rent due' (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 215.314(6)) and that the owner 'shall not discriminate against any household desiring to rent a subsidized unit on the basis of the' receipt of public assistance or voucher use (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 215.314(7)). The point is now stated in edu-no-subsidized-inspection-refusal-rule-mo (batch-9). Proposed sentence for edu-source-of-income-mo at sync: "If your multifamily project received additional subsidies from the Missouri Housing Development Commission, your agreement must bar you from refusing housing vouchers or Section 8 certificates in partial payment of rent and from discriminating against households that use them or receive public assistance." Basis: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 215.314(6)-(7), read whole 2026-11-09, single version (1989 H.B. 378).
- (for information) Mo. Rev. Stat. § 534.300: the unlawful detainer chapter 'shall not extend to any person' who 'has continued three whole years in the peaceable possession after the time for which the premises were demised or let' (read whole 2026-11-09). edu-eviction-process-mo and edu-holdover-damages-mo do not mention this three-year bar; batch-2's limitation row cites it. Consider one sentence in edu-eviction-process-mo at sync ("Unlawful detainer is not available against a holdover who has stayed in peaceable possession for three whole years after the lease ended; another action is needed"). Not a defect in either row.

### §7 Findings for other states

- Shared row lock-change-ak (AK;SC;UT, now MO) carries the title "Lock Changes (Alaska)", so every tagged state shows an Alaska label; its body is generic. Suggest a neutral title at sync (uniform change).

**batch-1**
- landlord-self-cure-as (shared AS;MP;NJ, now MO): the clause has the tenant pay the reasonable cost of repair or replacement work the landlord does. Taylor's 2026-10-20 rule (SOP rule 54 [MS 6]) says an optional clause that has the tenant pay toward repairs states that the repairs and parts paid for belong to the landlord and may not be removed. Check centrally whether the rule reaches self-cure costs; if it does, the added sentence is a uniform, landlord-protective shared edit (rule 62) for every tagged state. Missouri's tag does not depend on it.

**batch-4**
- KS and NE are tagged on both ev-charger-installation-hi and ev-charging-end-of-tenancy-co. If the landlord picks the hi clause's end-of-lease option "leave the system in place, and it will belong to Landlord", the co clause's "A charging system installed at Tenant's expense remains Tenant's property" contradicts it. Suggest the co clause carry a saving phrase ("Unless another Section of this Lease provides otherwise") or that those states keep one of the two.

**batch-7**
- automatic-renewal-ks (KS;SC;PA;AL): its single {{renewal_notice_days}} non-renewal notice also governs a month-to-month renewal; in a state whose month-to-month notice is a statutory minimum, a builder figure below that minimum would shorten it. The KS notes say to set it at or above the periodic notice, but that condition sits only in notes (rule 26 [NV-gaps 1]). Central check: add a sentence routing a month-to-month renewal to the statutory notice, as automatic-renewal-mo does.
- parking-rules-notice-co (CO;SC) carries rule_type REQUIRED from Colorado's statute; for a state tagged as optional (SC) the shared rule_type may misdescribe it. Central check.
