### Source requests still open

- FEDERAL: 34 U.S.C. § 12491 (VAWA): optional; the DV rows state Missouri law and only point to federal rules.

### Proposed SOP changes

- Battery tool defaults: make 1,200 characters the default window and run the start-anchored section-wide AND automatically in the same call, so a narrow window can't be cited alone (MO: round 1 widened about 180 absence citations; every conclusion held, but the work was avoidable). Also warn when a --possec section doesn't contain the pattern's words before running (about 30 failed-positive reruns across nine helpers) and when a pattern contains an inner `.{0,N}` cap.
- Quote checker: flag any single-quoted span that contains an apostrophe or ends mid-word, instead of silently re-pairing quotes (batch-1 helper found five passages the checker had passed).
- Validator: accept the "G<n> (recorded as failed, rerun as G<m>)" form and verify G<m> passed; refuse a failed battery cited any other way (adopted mid-pass for MO).
- CSR and other PDF-only sources: the built-in browser can read a sos.mo.gov chapter PDF by loading pdf.js from cdnjs on a same-origin page and extracting the text layer; hash the extracted operative text (alphanumeric SHA-256) and the saved file, and record excerpts as excerpts. This replaced every "CSR not in the corpus" NEEDS_REVIEW in the MO pass.
- After any source is read mid-pass, sweep every delivered row for "not read / not in the corpus / requested" claims naming it before the independent check (MO: 11 stale claims found and fixed by script).
- A pointer cleanup must keep the original claim's strength ("read for this batch" is not "read whole for this row") (MO round 2 P1).
- A lot-tenancy floor like Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.060(4)(2) ('notwithstanding any written lease provision') belongs in the clause screen for every clause that lets a landlord end or not renew a tenancy, own or shared (MO: five Missouri clauses and one shared tag needed it).

**batch-1**
- check.py's federal citation pattern requires at least three characters before "U.S.C.", so "FEDERAL: 9 U.S.C. § 2" (a one-digit title) never resolves and any quote from it fails; widen `[^;)]{3,60}?` to `{1,60}?`. edu-binding-arbitration-mo paraphrases 9 U.S.C. § 2 for this reason.
- check.py silently skips a single-quoted passage that contains a possessive ("tenant's"): the opener regex cannot close on the apostrophe, so the whole span is never checked and produces no WARN. Add a WARN for any `'` that opens a span with no valid close before the next citation. Batch-1 found five such unchecked spans by script and rewrote them with "..." around the possessive.
- check.py's continuation-citation parser turns a hyphenated UCC number in a comma chain ("Mo. Rev. Stat. § 400.2A-207, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 400.2A-208") into "400.2" and raises "cited section not in corpus"; separate such citations with semicolons or fix the continuation regex to accept `[0-9]+[A-Z]?-[0-9]+`.
- The checker also rejects any mention of a failed battery id even when written "recorded as failed, rerun as Gn"; either accept that form or say in the brief that failed runs are named only in the notes file.

**batch-2**
- Battery tool: when a --possec positive fails, print which limb rejected it (pattern, --ctx window, or --secctx). Four of this batch's six failed runs (G59, G63, G75 and G32) failed only on a context or section limb, which the summary line doesn't distinguish from a pattern miss.
- check.py flags any "G<n>" token for a battery with a failed positive, even in the disclosure wording rule 19 prescribes ("recorded as failed, rerun as Gn"), so helpers must describe failed runs without their ids in rows. Either let the checker accept that phrase or tell helpers to keep failed-run ids only in the notes file (what this batch did).

**batch-3**
- tools/check.py raises ERROR on any mention of a battery whose positive failed, including the form the brief prescribes ("recorded as failed, rerun as Gn"), so rows cannot carry that disclosure; this batch moved it to the notes file and the record's log line. Suggest the checker accept the prescribed phrase, or the brief say to put it in notes-batch-N.md.
- tools/check.py's citation parser truncates a letter-bearing section number in a comma-joined list ("Mo. Rev. Stat. § 400.2A-504" parsed as 400.2); this batch joined such lists with semicolons instead.
- tools/mo.py battery has no --supersedes field (SOP rule 19 [ND-gaps 2]); reruns here name the run they replace in --desc.

**batch-4**
- check.py: a citation list joined by 'and' whose second section number carries a letter (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 400.2A-209 and Mo. Rev. Stat. § 400.2A-517) resolves the second key as 400.2 and raises 'cited section not in corpus'; cite_keys' continuation regex should accept the same characters as the first key ([0-9A-Za-z.-]).
- Helper emit step: besides single-quote parsing, flag any single-quoted span followed immediately by a letter (an apostrophe inside the quote such as consumer's), since the checker then silently re-pairs quotes; this batch caught two such spans by a script before saving.
- mo.py battery: a --secctx built from several lookaheads ((?=[\s\S]*A)(?=[\s\S]*B)) is re-scanned at every position by re.search and ran past two minutes; anchor such patterns with \A inside the tool, or accept a list of section-wide limbs.

**batch-5**
- Checker: tools/check.py raises ERROR 'cited source not saved in work/sources' for a CSR citation in a NEEDS_REVIEW row's notes even when the record carries needs_source for that rule. Helpers then have to describe the rule without its citation in notes (done in edu-utility-disconnection-notice-authorization-mo). Suggest the checker accept a CSR citation that also appears in the record's needs_source, as a WARN.
- Battery tool: no --supersedes option, so reruns record the superseded run only in --desc prose ('rerun of G153 ...'); rule 19 [ND-gaps 2] wants a supersedes field. Suggest adding --supersedes G<n> to mo.py battery.

**batch-6**
- check.py: a citation list continuation with a letter-suffixed section (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 400.2A-205 after another Mo. Rev. Stat. citation joined by a comma or "and") is parsed as 400.2 and reported "cited section not in corpus"; the continuation regex in cite_keys takes only digits after the dot. Workaround used: separate such citations with semicolons. Suggest allowing [0-9A-Za-z\-] in the continuation key.
- check.py: the battery-count check reads any "N hits" within 60 characters after a G id, so "G165 (recorded as failed, rerun as G166) and the widened G168 (whole-code search, 73 hits)" was flagged as G166 = 73. Suggest anchoring the count to the parenthesis directly after the id.
- Helpers (this batch three times) named a real positive from memory of what a section probably says (441.510, 441.645, 589.417) and the run failed; the SOP already says to read the unit first (MN-gaps 36). A tool option that prints the pattern and context matches for a --possec section before logging would catch it without spending a battery id.

**batch-8**
- mo.py battery: --secctx with two start-anchored lookaheads (`(?=[\s\S]*A)(?=[\s\S]*B)`) is retried at every offset by re.search and does not finish over the whole code; suggest anchoring secctx with re.match, or accepting a list of section-wide AND terms.
- Lesson (again): a --possec chosen without reading the section first (441.233 for an insurance word) failed G331; the brief's lesson 2 stands.

**batch-9**
- Battery tool: when --possec names a section, print a warning before the run if the section's text does not match the pattern limb, so a wrong real positive does not spend a G-number (four of this batch's reruns came from that).

### Proposed topic questions


**batch-1**
- binding-arbitration: Does the state arbitration act exclude contracts of adhesion from its validation of pre-dispute clauses, so a standard-form residential lease may fall outside it? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 435.350, "except contracts of insurance and contracts of adhesion".)
- exculpatory-clauses: Besides lease disclaimers, do statutes give the landlord immunity for removing a tenant's property under an abandonment procedure or at execution of a possession judgment, or for relying on a police notice of drug activity? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.065, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.040(2), Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.060(6), Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.040.)
- part5-nonwaivable: Has a recent act added a non-waiver rule for a sale-leaseback of a home, which reaches the lease back to the seller? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 442.920(6), 2026.)
- promises-to-repair: Does the consumer protection act name a "false promise" as an unlawful practice and reach leases of real estate, so an unkept repair promise is actionable even without a repair-promise statute? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 407.020(1), Mo. Rev. Stat. § 407.010(6).)

**batch-2**
- eviction-service-party: Does the state's address confidentiality program make the Secretary of State (or another officer) the participant's agent for service of process, and how does an eviction petition that must describe the premises fit with the program's address protections? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 589.663(2)(b), Mo. Rev. Stat. § 589.664.)
- dv-lockchange: Where the state has no victim lock-change statute, does its anti-lockout statute (removal or exclusion "without judicial process and court order") let a landlord change locks against a co-tenant abuser on the strength of a protection order excluding that person? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.233(1), Mo. Rev. Stat. § 455.045(2).)
- eviction-hardship-stay: Does the state bar local governments from imposing eviction moratoria? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.012, 2024.)

**batch-3**
- utility-deposit-return / security-deposit-use: does the deposit statute's withholding list name utility charges, and does the state's definition of rent (or a "not rent for this chapter" rule) keep landlord-billed utilities out of the rent ground? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.300(4)(1), Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.005(5), Mo. Rev. Stat. § 535.020.)
- late-fee-limit: does a self-storage or other non-dwelling statute in the state set a late-fee figure or say a late fee "is not interest", and is there any dwelling counterpart? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 415.417, self-storage only.)

**batch-4**
- telecom-access: Does the state release the incumbent phone carrier from its carrier-of-last-resort duty when an apartment owner grants an exclusive voice-provider arrangement or bills residents for another provider's voice service through rent, and must the owner then notify occupants? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 392.460(3), (6), (9).)
- portable-solar: Does the net metering law bar any consumer from operating a generator in parallel with the utility without written approval, so that a plug-in solar device needs utility approval and risks disconnection of the account holder? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 386.890(15).)
- ev-charging-requirements: Does the weights and measures law reach electrical charging stations, and is a landlord billing tenants by use a retail location? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 413.226(1)(1), (2).) Is a charging-station operator excluded from the electric utility definition? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 386.020(15)(c).)
- association-obligations-disclosure: Does a long lease (for example 20 years including renewal options) make the tenant a purchaser entitled to the condominium resale certificate? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 448.1-103(24), Mo. Rev. Stat. § 448.4-109.)

**batch-5**
- elevator-safety (new topic or a question under fire-code-standard): Does a state elevator safety act bind the owner or lessee of a residential building's elevator, with registration, a state certificate of inspection and a posted operating certificate? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 701.371, 'No owner or lessee shall operate any installation not registered with the board'; Mo. Rev. Stat. § 701.363 and Mo. Rev. Stat. § 701.375 by catchline, heading only, not read.)
- heating: Does a state boiler and pressure vessel act require periodic state inspection certificates for heating or hot water supply boilers in apartment buildings above a size, and exempt small buildings or cities with their own codes? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 650.230(2)(2), (3), (5), 'apartment houses of less than six families' exempt; Mo. Rev. Stat. § 650.260(2)(2); Mo. Rev. Stat. § 650.290.)
- fire-code-standard: Does an old statewide fire-escape statute still bind owners and lessees of multi-story residential buildings (tenement houses) regardless of local code adoption? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 320.010, 'All buildings heretofore erected shall be made to conform to the provisions of this chapter'.)

**batch-6**
- required-disclosures / statutory-forms: Does a sale-leaseback statute require the buyer, who becomes the landlord, to give the selling homeowner a prescribed warning before the sale-leaseback agreement, with a waiting period before title passes? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 442.920(3)-(4), 2026; 'No provision of this section shall be modified or waived by any agreement.' (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 442.920(6)).)
- notice-delivery-methods / actual-notice-method: Does a general statute supply a default service method for any statutory notice that names none, and does it reach a notice ending a periodic tenancy? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 1.190.)
- property-tax-rent-disclosure: Does a renter property tax credit let the revenue department require landlords to certify gross rent received? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 135.010(3).)

**batch-7**
- guarantor-renewal: Does a general surety statute let a guarantor force the landlord to sue the tenant first, on pain of discharge, and does it reach a lease guaranty? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 433.010, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 433.030, 'in any bond, bill or note'.)
- home-business: Does a statute make a home business lawful unless an 'agreement restricting the use of land' restricts it, so the lease must state the restriction? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 71.990(2)(1).)

**batch-8**
- animal-care-access: Does the animal-welfare statute treat anyone who feeds or shelters an animal at one place for a set number of days as its owner, so that a landlord who takes in a tenant's abandoned pet takes on the care duty? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 578.005(7), (9), three or more consecutive days.)
- eminent-domain: May the parties claiming a condemnation award file an agreement dividing it, so that a lease term allocating the award operates as that agreement? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 523.053(1).)
- other-landlord-facilities: Does an elevator safety act require the owner, agent or lessee of a residential building to hold a state inspection certificate and post the operating permit? (Missouri: Mo. Rev. Stat. § 701.363, Mo. Rev. Stat. § 701.375(2), Mo. Rev. Stat. § 701.380.)

**batch-9**
- tenant-insurance-claims: Does an accidental-fire statute (Mo. Rev. Stat. § 441.010) bar a landlord or its insurer from suing a tenant for an accidental fire, and does a lease covenant change that?
- service-animal-denial-penalty: Does the criminal penalty for denying a guide, hearing or service dog cross-reference the state's housing access section, or only its public-accommodation section?

# Topic gaps sync (Claude Code, 2026-11-12)

**Merge.** Base 2a94980: 231 new rows and 34 shared rows tagged, matching the log's count (265 records) and the running record's final delivery; no other row changed, and none of the 34 shared rows' text had changed since the base. The first sync session was lost to the usage limit before anything was written, so this sync started over. The corpus folder was no longer on the Desktop, so statutes were re-read live on revisor.mo.gov (one request at a time, cached): all 949 single-quoted Mo. Rev. Stat. quotations in the delta and the sync's edits match the section text. Four sit in the 2027 or in-force version of a two-version section (§§ 513.430, 513.475 and 565.225), and their rows quote both versions. CSR, court-rule and federal quotations rest on the pass's own check.

**§6 applied:**
- `early-termination-mo` (Missouri item 8(a)): flat one-month fee, override of `early-termination-ks`; MO untagged from `early-termination-ks`. Abandonment sentence as the main pass corrected it (§ 441.065, read whole).
